Findings recorded in `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Large tier for a real brainstorm → spec/plan cycle with the user before implementation starts — this is the first item all session that touches infrastructure outside the repo itself, not a chat-agreed bounded slice. **Next: brainstorm "Stand up real CI" properly with the user**, or pick a different Large-tier item if they'd rather start elsewhere (Blender mesh/geometry sync, native plugins, rendering shadows/PBR, the RenderGraph refactor, UI/networking pillar gaps, the package manager, the marketplace, or the 3rd-person adventure template all remain, each needing its own decomposition).

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-21 (**Studio recent-projects list — picker UI + tracking (PR #197)** — ninth and LAST pick from the Medium tier, closing it out entirely; continuing the pre-authorized overnight autonomous session (this item was implemented and merged directly by the coordinating session itself, not a dispatched fork — a fork attempted mid-run couldn't be spawned ("Fork is not available inside a forked worker"), so the coordinator picked it up in place; a task-reviewer subagent was still dispatched before merging, same as every non-fork-constrained entry). Closes the last-remaining half of the original "Studio has zero persisted preferences" backlog item (window size/panel layout shipped 2026-09-20, theme shipped 2026-09-21/PR #196 above) — "no tracking or picker UI exists ... needs a project-picker shown at startup with no project-dir arg." `app/studio_prefs.hpp` gains a persisted `recent_projects` list on `StudioPrefs` and `record_recent_project()` (move-to-front, dedupe-by-exact-match, capped at 10) plus a pure `should_show_recent_projects_picker(open_path_given, recent_projects)` helper — deliberately kept at the app tier rather than in `studio/`, since the decision needs `Options::open_path`, an app-level concern, mirroring the exact cross-tier split the theme and per-plugin-enable/disable features already established (`studio::StudioApp` only ever receives a plain `std::vector<std::string>`/`bool`, never an app-level type). **A real dedupe-correctness question resolved by tracing the actual call site, not assumed:** whether the same project opened once via a relative path and once via its absolute form would fail to dedupe — confirmed `app/run/editor.cpp` always canonicalizes via `weakly_canonical` before recording, so no such gap exists. `StudioApp` gains a dismissible "Recent Projects" startup window (shown only for a bare `mosaic editor` with no path argument and a non-empty list — an explicit path always skips it, even with recent entries) and a `reopen_requested()` getter; **switching projects deliberately does not happen in-process** (`StudioApp` has no mechanism to reload with an entirely different project's assets/plugins/scene set, correctly named as its own future design rather than half-built here) — instead, on exit, the editor resolves its own running executable via `readlink("/proc/self/exe", ...)` and `execl()`s straight into `mosaic editor <chosen-project>`, a full process relaunch (Linux-specific by construction; a portable equivalent is an explicit follow-up if ever needed). A `readlink` failure drops the reopen request and exits normally rather than crashing, matching the picker's own dismiss-and-continue philosophy; an `execl` failure after a successful `readlink` reports to stderr directly (log sinks are already torn down by then) and exits 1. **Honestly disclosed, not swept under the rug, same as this session's established practice:** the actual "click a recent project, watch it relaunch" interaction could not be verified live — no GUI-automation/input-injection tooling is available in this sandbox (a hard constraint carried forward from the hot-reload entry's own security-classifier incident) and nothing else can drive an ImGui click — so the `readlink`/`execl` code path itself was never exercised, live or otherwise; the dispatched reviewer was asked to scrutinize it with particular care instead (buffer handling against `readlink`'s non-null-termination contract, the `execl` argv shape checked against the real CLI11 subcommand parsing, every failure branch), and confirmed it correct. What WAS verified end-to-end via real `mosaic editor` process launches (background process + `SIGINT` + reading `studio.toml` back, no simulated input): opening a project records and persists it; reopening the same project moves it to the front rather than duplicating; and launching bare with a non-empty recent list runs a full tick session and exits cleanly without corrupting the persisted list. 9 new tests (tracking insert/move-to-front/dedupe/cap, all three `should_show_recent_projects_picker` truth-table branches, a `StudioPrefs` TOML round-trip); full unit binary: 767/768 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated). Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging — confirmed via `Glob` that `StudioApp`/`draw_ui` has never had direct unit tests anywhere in this codebase, so leaving the picker's own ImGui widget code untested is consistent precedent, not a new gap; confirmed the only production `StudioApp` construction site (`app/run/editor.cpp`) was fully updated with both new trailing constructor parameters; found no issues. **With this, the entire 🟡 Medium tier of `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s "Effort vs. impact" section is now shipped** — five items across two nights (Studio plugin authoring via MCP #193, per-plugin enable/disable + versioning #194, plugin live file-watching/hot-reload #195, Studio theme system + persistence #196, Studio recent-projects list #197). **Next: the frontier moves to the 🔴 Large tier** (needs its own decomposition, multi-slice — Blender mesh/geometry sync, native `.so`/`.dll` plugins, rendering shadows/PBR/spot lights, the RenderGraph refactor, UI/networking pillar gaps, the package manager, the public marketplace, a 3rd-person adventure template, and standing up real CI are all still open); pick one, brainstorm → decompose → confirm with the user before starting implementation, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-21 (**Studio theme system + persistence (PR #196)** — eighth pick from the Medium tier, continuing the pre-authorized overnight autonomous session (this item was self-reviewed by a dispatched task-reviewer subagent, back to the standard practice after the hot-reload entry's fork-can't-spawn-subagents constraint below). The "theme" half of the original "Studio has zero persisted preferences" backlog item, deliberately deferred when window size/panel layout shipped 2026-09-20 ("theme has no existing theme system to persist a setting for -- would be a new feature"; see that entry further down). `engine/render/render_device.hpp` (engine tier) gains a `UiTheme` enum (Dark/Light/Classic), a `theme` parameter on `init_ui()` (default `Dark`), and a new `set_ui_theme(UiTheme)` virtual hook (default no-op, matching every other UI hook's contract) for switching the live color scheme at runtime — deliberately just ImGui's own three built-in `StyleColorsDark/Light/Classic()` calls, no custom palette infrastructure the backlog item didn't ask for. The Vulkan backend (`modules/render_vulkan/vulkan_render_device.cpp`) applies both `init_ui`'s one-time startup theme and `set_ui_theme`'s live switch through one shared private `apply_ui_theme()` helper, so the two call sites can never drift into applying the enum-to-`StyleColorsX()` mapping differently — the same "one function, not two independently-maintained copies" discipline `reload_plugins()` established for the hot-reload entry below. **The same cross-tier dependency question the per-plugin enable/disable work already resolved came up again and was resolved the same way:** `studio::StudioApp` can't reference an app-level type, so the persisted choice lives entirely in `app/studio_prefs.hpp` as a plain-int `StudioTheme` enum (`ThemeDark`/`ThemeLight`/`ThemeClassic` — the same "reflection layer supports int, not enum class" convention `engine/particles/particle_emitter.hpp`'s `Easing` already established, not a new pattern), with `studio_theme_to_ui_theme()`/`ui_theme_to_studio_theme()` mapping functions at the app/engine boundary; `StudioApp` itself only ever holds/reports a `UiTheme`, never the app-level int. **A real correctness question resolved by reading the actual code, not by assuming a comment was accurate:** whether a `studio.toml` written before this feature shipped (no `theme` key at all) would default correctly or silently misbehave — confirmed by reading `foundation/serialization/reflect_value.hpp`'s `ReadVisitor::member` directly that an unmatched key leaves the target field completely untouched, so it retains `StudioPrefs::theme`'s own `= ThemeDark` in-class initializer; covered by a dedicated test using a hand-written pre-theme-field TOML fixture, not just an assumption. A new "Theme" menu in `StudioApp`'s main menu bar (next to the existing Window menu) shows all three options with the active one checkmarked, calls `device.set_ui_theme(...)` live, and updates a new `current_theme_` member; `app/run/editor.cpp` threads the loaded preference into both `init_ui()` and `StudioApp`'s constructor at startup, and reads `current_theme()` back at exit to persist into `StudioPrefs` alongside the existing window-size read-back. 10 new/updated tests (both mapping functions in both directions, the out-of-range-value fallback to Dark, the pre-theme-field back-compat case); full unit binary: 759/760 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated). **Verified end-to-end via two real `mosaic editor` launches with a sandboxed `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, deliberately without any GUI-automation/input-injection/window-manipulation tooling** — a hard constraint carried forward from the hot-reload entry below, after that run's live-display verification attempt tripped a security classifier warning on the whole task even though the code it shipped was fine: a default-theme session ran a full tick loop and persisted `theme = 0` on a clean `SIGINT` exit; a second launch pre-seeded `studio.toml` with `theme = 1`, confirming it loads and applies at startup and round-trips unchanged through a full session with no menu interaction (menu-click verification isn't reachable under that constraint — the reviewer confirmed by code inspection instead that the guard on `set_ui_theme` can never be live-but-unready when the menu is visible, since the menu only ever draws after `init_ui()` has already run). Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging: confirmed the tier contract holds (`studio/` never references the app-level `StudioTheme` int), the reflection back-compat claim, the live menu-switch reaching `ui_ready_`-gated ImGui correctly, and the mapping's fallback consistency in both directions; found no must-fix issues, only a minor, explicitly-not-required note that the Vulkan backend's `apply_ui_theme()` switch itself has no dedicated unit test (a trivial 3-way dispatch, unlike the swapchain-support functions this codebase already extracted specifically to unit-test Vulkan-adjacent pure logic — accepted as-is, not chased). **Next: no fixed arc anymore** — check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Medium tier for the next pick (only the Studio recent-projects list remains); this overnight session continues through it autonomously per the user's pre-authorization, then looks at Large-tier decomposition if there's runway left, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-21 (**Plugin live file-watching/hot-reload (PR #195)** — seventh pick from the Medium tier, continuing the pre-authorized overnight autonomous session, executed by a dispatched fork of the coordinating session (a fork cannot itself dispatch a task-reviewer subagent, so this entry was self-reviewed in place of that step, disclosed explicitly rather than silently skipped). Closes `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s "Plugin live file-watching/hot-reload (currently manual 'Reload Plugins')" item. New `PluginFileWatcher` (`studio/plugin_file_watcher.hpp`) is deliberately mtime-polling, not a real inotify/kqueue/ReadDirectoryChangesW backend: `plugins/` directories are tiny, so a non-recursive scan + `last_write_time` comparison once per editor frame is cheap enough, and it keeps this header-only/stdlib-only like the rest of `studio/`'s plugin machinery rather than adding a new dependency or per-platform code path for a feature this small. `poll(directory)` returns false on the very first call for a directory (baseline only, no false positive on startup) and false while nothing has changed; true the instant a `*.py` file is added, removed, or modified; switching the tracked directory re-baselines rather than comparing against a stale snapshot. `StudioApp::draw_ui` polls once per frame; a detected change reruns the same `plugin_host_.load()` the manual "Reload Plugins" menu item already triggers — **a real, if small, cleanup caught during implementation, not left for review**: the load-and-refresh-panels logic was previously duplicated at the initial-load and manual-reload call sites; both now share one `reload_plugins()` helper alongside the new hot-reload trigger, so all three stay in sync by construction. Hot-reload deliberately does not bypass the already-shipped per-plugin enable/disable flag ([[per-plugin enable/disable, PR #194]]): `disabled_plugin_files_` is unchanged by a hot-reload, so editing a disabled plugin's file on disk is still skipped, exactly like a fresh load would skip it — consistent with that feature's own accepted "fixed at construction, restart to pick up a mid-session toggle" limitation. 8 new `PluginFileWatcher` tests (baseline-only first call, unchanged, modified/added/removed files, non-`.py` files ignored, missing/non-directory path, directory-switch re-baselining); full unit binary: 756/757 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated). **Verified end-to-end against the real `mosaic editor` binary on this sandbox's own live display, not just unit tests**: launched against a fresh project with one plugin, appended a line to the plugin file while the editor process was actually running, and confirmed the log line (`plugin: plugins/ changed on disk, reloading`) fired automatically mid-session with no manual reload click, followed by a clean SIGINT exit (code 0). Self-review (in place of a dispatched reviewer) checked: the tier contract holds (`PluginFileWatcher` never references `app::ProjectManifest`); `poll()`'s directory-missing/directory-appears/directory-disappears transitions all degrade correctly to `PluginHost::load`'s own already-tested "not an error" contract; the double-poll on a manual "Reload Plugins" click (once at the top of `draw_ui`, once to re-baseline after the click) is redundant but not incorrect, just extra work in that one frame — noted, not fixed, since fixing it would add branching complexity for a one-frame cost. Classified **bounded** (existing Studio/plugin-machinery flow, no new dependency, well-scoped once the polling-vs-real-filesystem-notification question was settled by the backlog's own "Medium" sizing rather than a heavier `inotify` design) — no spec/plan doc; design and scoping done autonomously per the user's overnight pre-authorization. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** — check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Medium tier for the next pick (Studio theme system + persistence, or the recent-projects list remain); this overnight session continues through them autonomously per the user's pre-authorization, PR-by-PR, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-21 (**Per-plugin enable/disable + versioning (PR #194)** — sixth pick from the Medium tier, continuing the pre-authorized overnight autonomous session (an interim fork attempt on this same item hit a Claude session rate limit mid-implementation; the reset window had already passed by the time it was noticed, so the in-progress branch/diff was picked up and finished directly rather than re-forked). `InstalledPlugin` (`app/project/project.hpp`) gains an `enabled` bool (default `true`, so every existing project/manifest round-trips unchanged), persisted in `mosaic.toml`. `set_plugin_enabled(project_dir, name, enabled)` (`app/plugin/package_install.hpp`) toggles it without touching any files on disk — reversible, unlike `plugin_remove`, which deletes the package's files. **A real cross-tier design constraint, resolved the same way `resolve_plugins_dir` resolved it for project-dir-vs-CWD:** Studio's `PluginHost::load` (`studio/plugin_host.hpp`/`.cpp`) can't reference `app::ProjectManifest` directly (Studio sits below `app/` in the dependency direction — `app/run/editor.cpp` constructs and drives `StudioApp`, not the reverse), so it gained a plain `disabled_files` parameter (bare `*.py` filenames to skip — neither run nor reported as an error) instead; `app/project/project_io.hpp`'s new `resolve_disabled_plugin_files()` derives that list from the manifest and is threaded through `app/run/editor.cpp` into `StudioApp`'s constructor, a new `disabled_plugin_files_` member consulted by both the initial load and "Reload Plugins" — fixed at construction, so a plugin toggled via CLI/MCP while an editor session is already open needs a restart to take effect, an accepted, explicitly-documented limitation rather than a silent gap. **Versioning:** `available_plugin_updates(project_dir, source_dir)` / `PluginUpdate{name, installed_version, available_version}` compares each installed plugin's recorded version against the same package source `install_package` already resolves against — deliberately "differs from," not "is newer than" (plain string inequality, no semver parsing), documented as such in the CLI help, the MCP tool description, and the doc comment alike, so nothing overclaims version ordering it doesn't actually check. Surface: `mosaic plugin enable/disable/updates <name> <project>` (CLI, `app/cli/options.*` + `app/run/plugin.*`) and `plugin_enable`/`plugin_disable`/`plugin_check_updates` (MCP tools 40-42, `app/mcp/mcp_tools.*`) — both follow `plugin_install`/`plugin_remove`'s existing option-wiring and schema-reuse conventions exactly (`plugin_enable`/`plugin_disable` reuse `schema_plugin_remove()`; `plugin_check_updates` reuses `schema_plugin_list()`). **A real, non-nitpick gap caught by a dispatched reviewer before merge:** `resolve_disabled_plugin_files()` — the actual glue between the manifest's `enabled` flags and what `PluginHost` skips, i.e. the crux of whether disabling a plugin actually takes effect at editor startup — had zero direct unit test coverage, unlike every other new function in the diff and unlike its own sibling `resolve_plugins_dir` (tested in the same file). Fixed: three new cases in `tests/unit/app/project_resolve_test.cpp` mirroring `resolve_plugins_dir`'s existing pattern (mixed enabled/disabled entries returning only the disabled files, no installed plugins at all, a bare scene file / missing path both returning empty rather than erroring). Reviewer confirmed everything else sound: round-trip correctness, graceful failure for an unknown/never-installed plugin across CLI *and* MCP, full consistency with `plugin_install`/`plugin_remove` conventions, and the dependency-tier contract genuinely holding (`studio/plugin_host.hpp` never references `app::ProjectManifest` or `InstalledPlugin` — confirmed by reading the header directly, not by trusting the diff's own framing). 17 new tests; full unit binary: 748/749 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated). Verified end-to-end twice, independently: via the real `mosaic` CLI (install → disable → enable → disable-of-an-unknown-name correctly rejected → bump the source version → `plugin updates` correctly reports the mismatch) and via a real `mosaic mcp` session (`plugin_install`/`plugin_disable`/`plugin_enable`/`plugin_check_updates` round trip, all `ok:true` where expected). Classified **bounded** (existing plugin-tooling flow, well-scoped once the cross-tier dependency question was resolved the same way a prior entry already had) — no spec/plan doc; design and scoping done in-session per the user's overnight pre-authorization rather than confirmed live. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** — check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Medium tier for the next pick (plugin hot-reload, Studio theme system, or the recent-projects list all remain); this overnight session continues through them autonomously per the user's pre-authorization, PR-by-PR, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-20 (**Studio plugin (`.py`) authoring via MCP — MCP tools 37-39 (PR #193)** — fifth pick from the Medium tier, picked up directly (user pre-authorized an overnight autonomous session working through the Medium tier while they slept). Closes `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s "Studio plugin (`.py`) authoring via MCP" item: `plugin_script_write`/`plugin_script_read`/`plugin_script_list` let an agent author a project's `plugins/*.py` files (`MOSAIC_MENU_ITEMS`/`MOSAIC_PANELS` declarations) the same way a human does through Studio, distinct from the existing `plugin_list` (which lists installable packages in a package source, not a project's own authored plugins). **`plugin_script_write` validates BEFORE persisting**, per the backlog item's own framing ("needs its own validation (throwaway `PluginHost` load)"): the candidate source is written to a scratch directory containing only that one file, then run through a throwaway `mosaic::studio::PluginHost` bound to the live World/reflection/commands — mirrors `set_script`'s own already-established "throwaway compile-check against the real World" precedent (`ScriptEditorModel::apply` does the same), not a new risk class. Only on success is the source copied into the project's real `plugins/<name>.py`; a compile error or a malformed `MOSAIC_MENU_ITEMS`/`MOSAIC_PANELS` dict returns `ok:false` with the specific message and leaves the real directory completely untouched, mirroring `set_script`'s own "leave it unchanged on error" contract. **A real bug caught by a dispatched reviewer before merge, not a nitpick:** the initial write path didn't check `create_directories`'s error code or the `ofstream`'s post-write state before returning `ok:true` — a write to an unwritable `plugins/` (permissions, disk full, a stray non-directory file already at that path) would have silently reported success with nothing actually persisted, contradicting the tool's own advertised contract and diverging from `save_scene_file`'s existing precedent of checking both. Fixed to surface both failure modes as `ok:false`, with a new regression test (a plain file named `"plugins"` forces `create_directories` to fail with `ENOTDIR`, without needing root/chmod games). Reused `mosaic::app::is_safe_source_name` (already shared by `plugin_source_add`) for the plugin name, confirmed by the reviewer as sufficient for a single filename component. 10 new tests (valid write + menu-item/panel reporting, overwrite semantics, compile-error rejection leaving the real directory untouched, malformed-dict rejection, unsafe-name rejection without touching disk, the write-failure regression above, malformed-argument `McpToolError`, read-of-nonexistent, sorted list excluding non-`.py` files); full `[mcp]`-tagged suite: 122/122 cases, 568/568 assertions; full unit binary: 733/734 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated). Verified end-to-end via a real `mosaic mcp` session against a fresh project: wrote a valid plugin declaring `MOSAIC_MENU_ITEMS`, confirmed the reported menu item, listed it, read its source back verbatim, then attempted a syntax-broken plugin — rejected with the real Python `SyntaxError`, never written to disk, confirmed absent on a follow-up read. Classified **bounded** (existing MCP-tool-authoring flow, well-scoped once the backlog's own validation approach was already specified) — no spec/plan doc. Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging, who found and specified the fix for the write-failure bug above; every other check (path safety, scratch-directory cleanup on all exit paths, schema/description accuracy, layering, test quality) came back sound. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** — check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Medium tier for the next pick (per-plugin enable/disable + versioning, plugin hot-reload, Studio theme system, or the recent-projects list all remain); this overnight session is proceeding through them autonomously per the user's own pre-authorization, PR-by-PR, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-20 (**Fixed ComponentRegistry's storage-index waste (issue #25)** — fourth pick from the Medium tier, a tracked GitHub issue flagged "correctness-safe but should-fix post-v0.1" since it was first documented. `foundation::reflection::type_id<T>()` draws from one global counter shared by EVERY reflected type in the whole program, not just ECS components -- `ComponentRegistry`'s `storages_` vector was indexed directly by that shared id (sized to `max_component_id + 1`), so any non-component reflected type (`Vec3`, `Quat`, `PackageManifest`, `StudioPrefs`, and dozens more added across this very session) inflated the vector with null gaps every time one got a `TypeID` assigned via `TypeRegistry::register_type<T>()`. **A real course-correction during scoping, caught before writing any code, not after:** the obvious fix -- giving `ComponentTypeID` its own separate counter -- would have reintroduced a previously-fixed bug the code's own comment named explicitly: "#9 identity drift," where the ECS and the reflection system once had two diverging integer id spaces for the same C++ type, deliberately consolidated into one shared identity source specifically to eliminate that confusion. Investigated the full history (the original 2026-08-16 reflection-system-design spec) to confirm this before proposing anything, then presented both the naive (wrong) approach and the two safe alternatives the original issue comment itself suggested to the user for a pick. Landed on: keep `ComponentTypeID`/`component_type_id<T>()` completely untouched (still the one identity `ComponentAccess::reads()`/`writes()` and anything else outside `ComponentRegistry` relies on, zero drift risk) -- and instead index `storages_` by a brand-new, deliberately separate, purely-internal dense slot (`engine/ecs/component_registry.hpp`'s `detail::component_storage_slot<T>()`), assigned only the first time a component type is actually stored via `add`/`get`/`has`, never exposed outside this one file, never compared against `ComponentTypeID` anywhere. Preserves genuinely dense, vector-backed O(1) storage indexing (the rejected alternative -- switching `storages_` to a hash map -- would have avoided the same identity-drift risk too, but at a per-lookup hashing cost on a hot ECS path; the dense-slot approach keeps array-speed access with zero new lookup cost). 1 new test proving the actual fix, not just the mechanism in isolation: two component types stored back-to-back get consecutive dense slots even with an unrelated non-component reflected type's `type_id<T>()` call sandwiched between them -- on the old code this exact sequence would have left a one-slot gap, confirmed sound by a dispatched reviewer who verified the test's relative-delta assertion is immune to the shared test binary's own prior pollution of either counter. Classified **bounded** once the safe-approach question was resolved with the user -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. Full unit binary: 723/724 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated); full `ctest`: the 6 known `--host`/`--connect` flakes, zero new. Verified end-to-end via a real `mosaic mcp` session against the starter template's own 13-entity, 20+-component-type scene (component add/get, ticking with physics+scripts running) -- no regression in any component storage path, extra scrutiny warranted and applied given this touches core ECS infrastructure directly. Reviewed with explicitly elevated care for the same reason -- confirmed the function-local-static-per-template-instantiation pattern gives the same one-slot-per-T-forever guarantee `type_id<T>()` itself already relies on, confirmed the thread-safety comment's framing is accurate (not overclaimed) by comparing directly against `type_id.hpp`'s own actual contract, and ran a full repo-wide grep confirming `storages_` is genuinely private with no external code anywhere assuming the old index-equals-`ComponentTypeID` relationship. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** -- check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Medium tier for the next pick (Studio plugin authoring via MCP, per-plugin enable/disable, plugin hot-reload, Studio theme system, or the recent-projects list all remain); confirm with the user, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-20 (**Undo support for `set_script` (Studio + MCP)** — third pick from the Medium tier, closing a real, previously-known inconsistency: `ScriptEditorModel::apply()` (`studio/script_editor.hpp`) wrote `ScriptComponent.source` via a raw field mutation (`sc->source = source;`), bypassing the undo/redo `CommandStack` entirely -- every other Studio/MCP-driven component edit (the Inspector panel, the generic `set_component` MCP tool) already went through `SetComponentCommand`. Fixed by reusing that exact same generic mechanism rather than inventing a dedicated command type: on a successful compile, `apply()` now snapshots the entity's current full `ScriptComponent` value via `ComponentReflection::component_value()`, replaces just the `source` key in the snapshotted `Value`'s `Table`, and runs the result through a `SetComponentCommand` (`cmd->execute()`, then `commands.push_executed(std::move(cmd))` -- the same "already executed, just record it" pattern `set_component`'s own MCP tool already uses). A real, meaningful consequence of reusing the generic mechanism rather than a narrower one: undo now restores the entity's *entire* prior `ScriptComponent` snapshot, not just `source` -- `path`/`script` (an `AssetID`) round-trip losslessly through the same reflection encode/decode path every other component edit already relies on, confirmed by a dispatched reviewer tracing `foundation/serialization/reflect_value.hpp`'s actual `encode`/`decode` implementations for both field types rather than assuming losslessness. Only two call sites needed updating (`studio_app.cpp`'s Script Editor panel, `mcp_tools.cpp`'s `tool_set_script`), both of which already had a `CommandStack` in scope -- `apply()` simply gained a new required parameter. Verified end-to-end via a real `mosaic mcp` session, not just unit tests: `set_script` on a fresh `ScriptComponent`, `undo_depth` correctly reporting `1`, `undo` restoring the prior (empty) source, `redo` re-applying the edit -- all through the actual `undo`/`redo`/`undo_depth` tools an agent would really call. Classified **bounded** (existing flow, single well-understood mechanism to reuse) -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. 6 new/updated tests: an undo→redo round trip via `ScriptEditorModel` directly, a `path`/`script`-fields-survive-undo test using a real non-default `path` (not a vacuous empty-string check, confirmed meaningful by review), the pre-existing rejected-compile tests now also asserting the undo stack stays at depth 0, and a full round trip through the real MCP `set_script`/`undo`/`redo` tools specifically (not just Studio's direct-call layer). Full unit binary: 722/723 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated); full `ctest`: the 6 known `--host`/`--connect` flakes, zero new. Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging -- confirmed `cmd->valid()`'s false-branch is effectively unreachable defensive code here (same accepted pattern the pre-existing `tool_set_component` already uses, not a new anti-pattern) and confirmed the new `CommandStack&` parameter's position in `tool_set_script`'s signature correctly follows the generic `tool_handlers()` ordering convention rather than the special-cased `tool_import_asset`/`tool_build_project` shape (which don't need `World&`/`CommandStack&` at all, for unrelated reasons). **Next: no fixed arc anymore** -- check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Medium tier for the next pick (Studio plugin authoring via MCP, per-plugin enable/disable, plugin hot-reload, TypeID densification, Studio theme system, or the recent-projects list all remain); confirm with the user, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-20 (**Agent API — `entities_near`/`scene_bounds`, MCP tools 35-36** — second pick from the Medium tier, the highest-rated remaining item (Medium-High) after the plugin discovery fix below shipped. "Spatial queries" -- no distance/containment query machinery existed anywhere in the engine before this. **A real staleness trap identified and designed around during scoping, not discovered later:** the obvious approach (read the cached `WorldTransform` component) would have been silently wrong -- `TransformSystem` is deliberately NOT part of `World`'s regular system list (it's derived/render-time state, run manually by `app/run/editor.cpp`'s own frame loop, not simulation), so a headless `mosaic mcp` session only ever gets `WorldTransform` populated once at startup and never again; any entity moved mid-session, or newly created, would have a stale or entirely absent cached value. Solved by computing each entity's world position **live, on every query call**, via `TransformSystem::compute_world()` -- the exact same recursive parent-chain composer `TransformSystem::update()` itself already uses internally to produce `WorldTransform` in the first place -- rather than duplicating that composition logic. `compute_world` (and its `kMaxDepth` cycle guard) were `private static`; made public with zero behavior change, reused as-is instead of reinvented. `entities_near(x, y, z, radius)`: every entity with a `Transform` within `radius` of the given point, nearest-first, with real position and distance; rejects a negative radius as `McpToolError` rather than silently returning nothing. `scene_bounds()`: the point-cloud min/max corners over every positioned entity plus `entity_count` -- explicitly documented as NOT accounting for mesh geometry extents (an entity's actual rendered footprint can extend beyond the box), since computing true geometry-aware bounds would need mesh-vertex access, a materially bigger task the original spec's own framing ("needs `WorldTransform` positions") never actually called for. Both tools share a new `positioned_entities(World&)` helper reused identically by each, so Transform-less-entity exclusion is applied consistently. Verified end-to-end against the real starter template's own 11 positioned entities via a real `mosaic mcp` session: a sane bounding box, correctly nearest-first-sorted matches, and a negative radius correctly surfaced as an error rather than silently clamped. Classified **bounded** (existing MCP-tool-authoring flow, well-scoped once the staleness trap was identified and designed around) -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. 8 new tests, including one that specifically proves live computation by asserting no `WorldTransform` component exists on either entity at all in a parent-child pair, yet the composed world position is still correct; full `[mcp]`-tagged suite: 111/111 cases, 508/508 assertions; full unit binary: 719/720 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated); full `ctest`: the 6 known `--host`/`--connect` flakes, zero new. Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging -- confirmed the `TransformSystem` visibility change didn't accidentally expose anything else, confirmed the `Mat4` translation-extraction indices (`m[12..14]`) match the documented column-major layout and the identical existing precedent in `render_system.hpp`, and noted one minor **pre-existing** (not introduced here) test-coverage gap: no test anywhere exercises `compute_world`'s cycle guard directly, for these new tools or the original `TransformSystem::update()` path either -- flagged as a good follow-up, not a blocker. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** -- check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Medium tier for the next pick (Undo support for `set_script`, Studio plugin authoring via MCP, per-plugin enable/disable, plugin hot-reload, TypeID densification, Studio theme system, or the recent-projects list all remain); confirm with the user, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-20 (**Studio — plugin discovery now follows the project dir, not CWD** — first pick from the Medium tier after three Quick-wins re-derivations in a row came back dry (entry below); recommended by the user as the highest-leverage Medium item since the backlog itself flagged it Medium-High, "a silent correctness bug for any packaged distribution." Confirmed real: `studio/studio_app.cpp`'s `PluginHost` loaded from a bare relative `"plugins"` literal, resolved against whatever the process's CWD happened to be at runtime -- broken for any launch whose CWD isn't the project root (a desktop shortcut, a packaged distribution's launcher, `mosaic editor /path/to/project` run from elsewhere), and silently so, since a missing directory is "no plugins found," not a load failure, by `PluginHost::load`'s own existing contract. `app/asset/asset_io.hpp`'s separate `import_plugins` mechanism (used by `mosaic run`) already correctly resolved `<project_dir>/plugins` -- only Studio's own `PluginHost` had this gap, confirmed by a dispatched reviewer reading that file directly rather than trusting the claim. New `resolve_plugins_dir()` (`app/project/project_io.hpp`, alongside the existing, already-tested `resolve_startup_scene()`) derives `<project_dir>/plugins` from `Options::open_path` (the CLI's positional project-dir/scene-file argument), nullopt when that argument isn't actually a directory (a bare scene file, or nothing given) -- `StudioApp` then falls back to its constructor's prior CWD-relative default for that one case, unchanged behavior. `Options::plugins_dir` computed in `app/main.cpp` the same place `assets_dir` already is; `StudioApp`'s constructor gains a defaulted trailing `plugins_dir` parameter (default `"plugins"`, so every existing/test call site keeps compiling and behaving identically) threaded to both `plugin_host_.load()` call sites. **Verified end-to-end via the real `mosaic editor` binary in a way that leaves no room for doubt, not just unit tests or a plausible-looking diff:** created a project with an intentionally-syntax-broken `.py` file at `<project>/plugins/broken_plugin.py`, launched `mosaic editor <project>` from a completely unrelated working directory, and confirmed via the logged `SyntaxError` (tagged `"plugin"`, `PluginHost`'s own error channel, not `import_plugins`'s separate `"import"`-tagged one -- a dispatched reviewer confirmed there's no ambiguity about which mechanism produced it) that the plugin was genuinely found and parsed from the project's real `plugins/` directory -- the exact bug scenario, directly observed rather than inferred. **A real doc-staleness bug caught by review, not a nitpick:** `docs/design/scripting.md` had a whole section titled "Where the plugins directory lives (and its known gap)" describing this exact bug as an unscheduled future follow-up -- left in place, it would have been factually backwards the moment this merged (describing an already-fixed gap as still-open); rewritten to describe the actual fix. Classified **bounded** (existing flow, well-scoped fix once the exact broken call sites were identified) -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. 2 new tests for `resolve_plugins_dir`; full unit binary: 714/715 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated). Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging -- confirmed no other bare `"plugins"` literal or CWD-relative path shares this bug anywhere else in the codebase via an independent grep, not just trusting the commit's own claim. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** -- check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s Medium tier for the next pick (Spatial queries, undo support for `set_script`, Studio plugin authoring via MCP, per-plugin enable/disable, plugin hot-reload, TypeID densification, Studio theme system, or the recent-projects list all remain); confirm with the user, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-20 (**Agent API — confirmed AssetID referencing via `set_component` already works, no code needed** — after File → Reload shipped (entry below) and its arc closed, the Quick wins tier was re-derived a second time today; the only genuine new candidate was this one, an old spec's Non-goals note (`2026-09-18-asset-generation-import-asset-design.md`) that had speculated "attaching an already-imported asset to a component field is arguably already possible today through the existing generic `set_component` tool... worth confirming and documenting with a real test, but not designed further here" -- never actually verified until now. Traced the mechanism through the code: `ComponentReflection::set_value`'s default `ReferenceResolver` (`foundation/serialization/reference.hpp`) has an identity `resolve_asset` (returns the given `AssetID` unchanged, no registry lookup at set-time) -- so a bare JSON UUID string written into an `AssetID` field via `set_component` is accepted verbatim, exactly as speculated, with validity checking deferred entirely to read-time tools like `broken_references` (by design, not a gap). **Turned out to already be extensively proven, just never called out as its own closed item:** `tests/unit/app/mcp_tools_test.cpp`'s own `broken_references`/`orphaned_assets` test suites already use `set_component` on `Material.texture` as their own test setup, with both a stray (unregistered) id and a real registered one, in multiple test cases each. Re-confirmed once more end-to-end via the real `mosaic mcp` CLI for full certainty: imported a real PPM asset, `set_component`'d `Material.texture` to its returned id, `get_component` read it back correctly, and `broken_references` did not flag it (only the pre-existing, unrelated starter-template `AudioSource.clip` gap showed up). Historical spec file left untouched (dated point-in-time artifact, per this session's established convention) -- the confirmation is recorded in `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md` instead, added and immediately marked confirmed in the same edit rather than tracked open first. **Zero code changes** -- this was a verification task, not a build task; no branch/PR/review cycle, matching the file-fix precedent from earlier today. **Next: no fixed arc anymore, and Quick wins are empty again after two re-derivations in one day** -- worth checking with the user whether a third re-derivation is warranted immediately or whether to move to a Medium-tier item instead; either way, confirm the pick with the user, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-20 (**Studio — "File → Reload" DONE, plus two new engine/foundation primitives it required** — picked up "In-editor File → Reload" from the entry below after the doc fix shipped; confirmed borderline Quick/Medium during scoping, and it did in fact turn out bigger than a pure-UI quick win. Scoping investigation found Studio had no way to synchronously replace a live scene: `World::destroy_entity()` is deferred until the next `tick()` (which also runs every system -- not a clean "just clear it" operation), and `CommandStack` had no way to drop undo/redo history at all. Two new primitives closed both gaps: `World::clear()` (`engine/world/world.hpp`/`.cpp`) synchronously destroys every entity via the same per-entity teardown (`ComponentRegistry::on_destroy`) every ordinary destroy already uses, but immediately and without stepping any system; `CommandStack::clear()` (`foundation/commands/command_stack.hpp`) drops both stacks without invoking `undo()`/`redo()`. `reload_scene_from_disk()` (`app/run/scene_load.hpp`) is fail-safe by construction -- the target file loads into a throwaway scratch `World` FIRST purely to validate it parses; only on success does it clear the live `world` and load the same text into it for real, so a missing/malformed file leaves the current scene completely untouched (deliberately never falls back to the demo scene the way `populate_world` does -- Reload either succeeds at the named file or changes nothing). `SceneFileModel` gained `request_reload()`/`consume_reload_request()`, mirroring the existing save-request pattern exactly. `StudioApp::on_scene_reloaded()` (new) resets every Studio-side cache naming an entity from the now-replaced scene -- undo history, selection, the demo-physics toggle target, the Script Editor's current target -- confirmed against every one of `StudioApp`'s private members by a dispatched reviewer, who also independently confirmed `ScriptSystem`'s own per-entity engine cache self-heals on the next tick (prunes anything not revisited) with no special handling needed, and that `PhysicsSystem`'s equivalent gap is pre-existing and unrelated to this change. **A real bug caught by review, not a nitpick:** the confirmation popup (shown when reloading a dirty scene, since discarding unsaved edits is a one-way action) called `ImGui::OpenPopup` from inside the File menu while `ImGui::BeginPopupModal` was called after `EndMainMenuBar()` -- different ImGui window/ID-stack contexts, a well-documented Dear ImGui pitfall (ocornut/imgui #331/#1134/#1422/#5684) that would have made the popup silently never open, so clicking Reload on a dirty scene would have appeared to do nothing. Fixed with ImGui's own documented workaround (a bool flag consumed immediately before `BeginPopupModal`, same call-site depth) and confirmed correct by a second, focused review pass citing Dear ImGui's own demo idiom -- this is exactly the class of bug a launch-only smoke test can never catch, only interaction (or, lacking that here, careful review against the library's actual documented contract). **Honestly disclosed, not swept under the rug:** the interactive click-through itself still isn't verifiable in this sandbox -- no input-injection tooling drives this specific Wayland window, and there's no way to screenshot to confirm visually even if it could; correctness rests on the unit tests (reload logic) and on matching Dear ImGui's documented contract exactly, confirmed twice independently (popup logic), not on having watched it render. Classified **bounded**, upgraded in scope mid-design once the missing primitives were identified -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. 11 new tests across `World`, `CommandStack`, `SceneFileModel`, and `reload_scene_from_disk`; full unit binary: 712/713 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated); full `ctest`: the 6 known `--host`/`--connect` flakes, zero new (the threaded-script flake didn't reproduce that run). Regression-verified the real `mosaic editor` binary launches/runs/exits cleanly, both before and after the popup fix. Reviewed twice by dispatched task reviewers before merging -- the first found the popup bug, the second confirmed the fix. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** -- check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s "Effort vs. impact" section for the current highest-leverage pick; confirm with the user, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-20 (**Studio — persisted window size + relocated panel layout** — picked from `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s 🟢 Quick wins tier ("Studio: persisted preferences"), confirmed with the user after the CLI-verb-wrapping arc closed below. Deliberately no default argument on the override itself -- repeating a base class's default on a virtual override is a known C++ footgun (dispatch through a base pointer/reference uses the STATIC type's default, not the dynamic one), confirmed as the right call by a dispatched reviewer who also checked the only real call site always passes the argument explicitly anyway. `user_config_dir()` -- previously wedged inside `app/plugin/package_sources_registry.hpp`, this session's earlier `plugin_source_*` work -- extracted into its own tiny `app/user_config_dir.hpp` so studio prefs doesn't need an awkward cross-domain include just for one path helper; confirmed by review that nothing else relied on getting it transitively. **Verified against the REAL `mosaic editor` binary on this sandbox's own live display, not just unit tests** -- a first this session, since prior GUI work could only be checked via exit-code/stderr, never an actual running window: launched with `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` sandboxed, ran ~3.4s, shut down cleanly via `SIGINT` (exit 0, clean logs), confirmed `studio.toml`/`studio_layout.ini` landed under the sandboxed config dir, and a second launch read back and re-wrote the identical window size, proving the round trip actually works end-to-end; separately confirmed the real `~/.config/mosaic/` was never touched by either run. Classified **bounded** once the slice-scoping question was resolved with the user -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. 4 new tests (`studio_prefs_test.cpp`: missing-file defaults, save/load round-trip, unparseable-file defaults, save creates a missing parent dir); full unit binary: 705/706 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated); full `ctest`: the 6 known `--host`/`--connect` flakes plus the same threaded-script flake, zero new. Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging, including a careful check of the `io.IniFilename` pointer-lifetime handling (safe: the `ui_ready_` re-entry guard runs before the member string is ever touched, so a hypothetical second `init_ui()` call can't reassign the string out from under a pointer ImGui already cached) and the layering implications (none -- a plain `std::string` parameter carries no symbol-level Engine→App coupling). **Next: no fixed arc anymore** -- theme and recent-projects remain open as separate future slices of the same original backlog item if ever picked up; check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s "Effort vs. impact" section for the current highest-leverage quick win before starting anything new; confirm the pick with the user, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-19 (**Agent API — `tick_until`, a 27th MCP tool** — picked from `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s 🟢 Quick wins tier ("tick until condition met" tool), confirmed with the user after the pre-commit hook entry below shipped. Closes a real gap the existing `tick` tool left open: an agent driving `tick` in a loop and calling `get_component` after every single call to check a condition, versus one call that ticks exactly as many times as needed. Given `entity`/`component`/`field`/`op`/`value` (the identical shape `entities_where` already established) plus optional `max_ticks` (default/hard-cap 10000, same cap `tick` itself already uses) and `dt` (default 1/60), it ticks one at a time, rechecking the field after each tick, stopping the instant the condition is met or `max_ticks` is exhausted. Checks *before* ticking at all, so an already-satisfied condition costs zero ticks. Reuses `entities_where`'s exact matching primitives (`compare`/`json_at_path`/`valid_compare_ops`) and its established convention that an unresolvable entity/component/field never throws -- it just never matches, so the call simply runs to `max_ticks` and reports `met: false`. A real bug caught and fixed during implementation, not just in review: the first draft collapsed "field never resolved" and "field resolved to an actual JSON `null`" into the same sentinel value, which would have made `op: "eq", value: null` permanently unsatisfiable -- fixed by using `std::optional<nlohmann::json>` throughout instead of a null-as-sentinel, confirmed correct by a dispatched reviewer (though, per that same review, not directly proven by a test -- no currently-reflected component field naturally produces a resolved `null`, the identical untested edge `entities_where` itself already carries). Fits the uniform `ToolHandler` shape directly (only needs `World&`) -- no `handle_tool_call` wiring changes. Verified end-to-end against a real `mosaic mcp` session, not just synthetic tests: a script incrementing `Transform.position.x` each tick, `tick_until` stopping at exactly `ticks:5` when `position.x` reached `5.0`, and correctly running to `max_ticks:3` (landing at `value:8.0`, `met:false`) for a target it couldn't reach in time. Classified **bounded** (existing flow, well-scoped addition on already-proven `entities_where`/`tick` machinery) -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. 6 new tests (already-met/zero-tick, real multi-tick convergence via a live `ScriptComponent`, unmet-after-cap, unresolvable-entity, `max_ticks`-exceeds-limit error, unknown-op error); full `[mcp]`-tagged suite: 93/93 cases, 404/404 assertions; full unit binary: 688/689 (the one known pre-existing threaded-ScriptSystem flake, unrelated). Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging, same as every change this session. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** -- check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s "Effort vs. impact" section for the current highest-leverage quick win before starting anything new; confirm the pick with the user, same practice as every entry this session.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-19 (**Tooling — pre-commit hook catches stale generated docs/nav** — picked from `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s 🟢 Quick wins tier ("CI/pre-commit wiring for `mosaic docs` + nav regen"), confirmed with the user as the next pick after the field-labels doc entry below. Real discovery mid-task: this repo has **no CI at all** (no `.github/workflows/` anywhere, despite `CONTRIBUTING.md` claiming dependency-rule violations are "enforced in CI" -- they aren't, that's a separate pre-existing gap not touched here), so "CI/pre-commit wiring" had nothing to wire into; scope was narrowed with the user to a local pre-commit hook only, standing up actual CI deferred as a bigger, separate follow-up. `.githooks/pre-commit` (bash) regenerates `docs/reference/components.md`/`scripting-globals.md` via `mosaic docs` (skips gracefully with a warning, doesn't block, if no built binary is found at `build/{,debug/,release/}app/mosaic` -- a fresh clone shouldn't be forced to build first) and `docs/index.html`'s nav sections via the existing `tools/docs/regenerate_nav.py` (always runs, no build needed), then `git diff --exit-code`s the affected files -- any diff means regeneration changed something, so the commit is blocked with a message pointing at the now-refreshed working-tree files to review and `git add`. One-time opt-in via `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, documented in `CONTRIBUTING.md`. **Caught a real bug on its very first run, not a contrived test case:** `docs/index.html`'s AUTO-GENERATED specs/plans nav sections hadn't been regenerated since 2026-09-16, silently missing ~10 specs/plans from everything built since (the entire project-health/bulk-editing arc and more) -- fixed in the same branch, directly confirming the backlog's "already bitten this project twice" framing. Two known limitations surfaced by a dispatched reviewer and documented in both the hook's header comment and `CONTRIBUTING.md`: (1) the reference-doc check only reflects whatever `mosaic` binary happens to be built, not whether it's newer than the change being committed -- editing a reflected component and forgetting to rebuild before committing passes silently; (2) `git config core.hooksPath .githooks` *replaces*, not augments, any hooks a contributor already has under `.git/hooks/`. Classified **bounded** after the CI-scope question was resolved with the user -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. No new automated test suite for the hook itself (it glues together two already-tested generators -- `mosaic docs`'s own suite, `tools/docs/test_regenerate_nav.py`'s 11 tests, both confirmed still green) -- verified instead via real manual end-to-end runs: a stale-docs commit correctly blocked (the real nav fix above), a clean commit passed with zero diff (confirming idempotency), and the no-build-found path skips without blocking. Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging, same as every change this session. **Next: no fixed arc anymore** -- check `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`'s "Effort vs. impact" section for the current highest-leverage quick win before starting anything new; confirm the pick with the user, same practice as this entry.)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-19 (**Agent API — hot-reload imported assets into the live `AssetRegistry`** — standalone bug fix, not a new tool. Picked directly from `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`, hit and worked around three separate times during the just-finished project-health arc (`broken_references`, `orphaned_assets`, and implicitly `entities_referencing`'s own future). `import_asset` previously only wrote a new asset to disk and its metadata -- the live `AssetRegistry` a `mosaic mcp` session actually reads from was only populated once at startup, so every same-session import was invisible to `broken_references`/`orphaned_assets`/`entities_referencing` until the process restarted, a caveat all three tools' own `tools_list_schema()` descriptions had to carry. Fixed by threading a NEW, separately-defaulted `AssetRegistry* live_asset_registry = nullptr` parameter through `handle_tool_call` -- deliberately a second parameter, not a constness change to the existing `const AssetRegistry&` one, since a mutable reference can't default-bind to a temporary the way the const one can; every pre-existing call site keeps compiling and behaving unchanged. On a successful import, `tool_import_asset` reruns the exact same `load_project_assets()` loader already used at startup against the project's `assets/` directory -- idempotent (`AssetRegistry::register_asset` overwrites by uuid), so rerunning the trusted whole-directory scan is simpler and safer than writing new single-asset loading logic that could desync from the startup path. `app/run/mcp.cpp`'s dispatch passes `&asset_registry` (the same object already passed by const reference) as the new pointer, so both parameters always refer to one identical registry for the session's lifetime. Classified **bounded** (existing flow, well-scoped fix) during brainstorming -- design agreed in chat, no spec/plan doc. Manually verified end-to-end against a real `mosaic create`d project via the actual CLI, directly reversing what was previously (correctly) documented as impossible: imported a PPM in one continuous `mosaic mcp` session, then immediately called `orphaned_assets` and saw the just-imported asset appear (previously would have been completely absent); separately set a `Material.texture` to that same id and confirmed `broken_references` did NOT flag it (previously would have false-positived). Updated all three affected tools' schema descriptions to drop the now-stale staleness caveat. 2 new tests; full `[mcp]`-tagged suite: 79/79 cases, 363/363 assertions, all green; full `ctest`: 885/892, the 6 known `--host`/`--connect` flakes plus one other threaded-script test that failed only under full-suite ordering and passed cleanly in isolation (same known embedded-Python-GIL-teardown flake family as the previously-documented one, unrelated to this diff), zero new. Reviewed by a dispatched task reviewer before merging, same as every change this session.)

Answers "what entities/components/fields reference this specific asset id" -- the third variant of a `FieldVisitor`-subclass walk pattern this session proved twice already: `AssetRefCollector` (`broken_references`) checks each field against an `AssetRegistry`; `ReferencedAssetGatherer` (`orphaned_assets`) collects every referenced id into a set; the new `AssetReferenceCollector` does neither -- it just checks `value == target` for one caller-given `AssetID` and records the match. ¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-19 (**Project health & bulk editing — `bulk_set_field` DONE, sub-project 4 of 4 — THE LAST ONE. The entire project-health/bulk-editing arc (from `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md`) is now COMPLETE.** A 25th MCP tool: sets one field to a new value on every entity matching an optional filter, reusing `entities_where`'s exact `where.field/op/value` shape and operators verbatim for selection (omit `where` to target every entity with `component`). Covers "mass rename" as the flagship use case (e.g. bulk-setting `Name.value`) rather than as a separate operation -- re-reading sub-project 1's original "mass rename/set-field/find-replace" framing during this spec's own brainstorming showed renaming is just set-field's most common use, not a distinct primitive; `find-replace` (substring pattern matching within a string field) remains explicit future work with its own unresolved design questions (case sensitivity, substring vs. whole-value matching), deferred to keep v1 tight like every prior sub-project. A new write-side `json_set_at` (`app/mcp/mcp_tools.cpp`) is the counterpart to `entities_where`'s read-side `json_at_path` -- deliberately requires every path segment including the leaf to already exist (a reflected component's fields are fixed by its C++ struct), so an unresolvable `set.field` is always a malformed request, never a per-entity condition. **Two-phase execution is the core safety property this tool exists to prove:** every matched entity's patch is selected and validated FIRST (in a `for_each_entity` walk that only builds local `matched`/`patched` vectors), and no `Command` is constructed or executed until every patch is known to succeed -- a `set.field` typo throws `McpToolError` before a single entity is touched, confirmed by a dedicated test asserting the world is byte-for-byte unchanged after a thrown error. **New generic foundation-tier primitive:** `CompositeCommand` (`foundation/commands/composite_command.hpp`, its own standalone-tested unit, Task 1 of this sub-project's plan) wraps N `Command`s as one atomic undo step -- `execute()` runs sub-commands in order, `undo()` reverses in reverse order -- so a bulk edit touching many entities reverts with exactly ONE `undo` call, not N separate ones; `CommandStack` had no batch/composite command type before this. Confirmed atomicity against the real CLI, not just unit tests: created two entities, bulk-renamed both in one `bulk_set_field` call, then a SINGLE `undo` call reverted BOTH entities' `Name.value` back to their prior value in the same continuous `mosaic mcp` session. Zero matches returns `{"updated": 0, "entities": []}` without touching `CommandStack` at all -- no no-op command pollutes the undo history. Fits the uniform `ToolHandler` shape directly (needs only `World&`/`ComponentReflection&`/`CommandStack&`, all already parameters) -- no `handle_tool_call` signature change, unlike `broken_references`/`orphaned_assets` which needed `AssetRegistry` threaded through. 11 new tests total (5 `CompositeCommand` unit tests + 6 `bulk_set_field` MCP tests); full `[mcp]`-tagged suite: 70/70 cases, 333/333 assertions, all green; full suite 876/883 (`ctest`), the same 7 known pre-existing `--host`/`--connect`/threaded-Python-teardown flakes, zero new. Both Task 1 and Task 2 implemented directly by Luci (no `agy` CLI delegation, per the process lesson from sub-project 2's incident) and reviewed by dispatched task-reviewer subagents. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-19-project-health-bulk-set-field-design.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-19-project-health-bulk-set-field.md`. **With this, "bulk refactoring / project health checks" in `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md` is fully shipped: all 4 sub-projects (`entities_where`, `broken_references`, `orphaned_assets`, `bulk_set_field`) complete. The `mosaic mcp` server now has 25 tools.**)

¶ **Last updated:** 2026-09-19 (**Project health & bulk editing — `orphaned_assets` DONE, sub-project 3 of 4** — the third slice of the arc picked from `docs/project/deferred-work-backlog.md` (sub-project 2, `broken_references`, shipped and merged first; see its own entry below). A new CLI subcommand runs a headless MCP (Model Context Protocol) server -- the same protocol this development session uses to reach other tools -- exposing entity/component CRUD + undo/redo/save over stdio JSON-RPC, so an agent gets a validated, undoable editing surface instead of raw file edits that bypass `ComponentReflection`/`CommandStack` entirely. `app/mcp/mcp_tools.hpp`/`.cpp` is a near-exact mirror of `world_globals.cpp`'s existing Python scripting surface (`create_entity`, `destroy_entity`, `get_component`, `set_component`, `add_component`, `remove_component`, `has_component`, `component_names`, `entities`, `entities_with`, `entity_count`, `undo`, `redo`, `undo_depth`, plus a new `save_scene`) but returns/accepts `nlohmann::json` instead of `py::object` -- the same architectural move `install_imgui_globals` made for a different transport, just applied here to JSON-RPC instead of pybind11, with **zero Python/pybind11 involvement anywhere in this feature**. `app/mcp/mcp_protocol.hpp`/`.cpp` is an engine-agnostic JSON-RPC 2.0/stdio framing layer (hand-rolled with the already-vendored `nlohmann_json`, matching this project's "own small implementations, minimal deps" style) implementing MCP's `initialize`/`tools/list`/`tools/call`, driven via an injected `ToolDispatch` callback so it's independently testable with a stub. `mosaic mcp <project-dir>` (`app/run/mcp.hpp`/`.cpp`) resolves the project exactly as `mosaic editor` does (`load_manifest` + the manifest's `default_scene`, failing fast on a missing `mosaic.toml`), builds a resident `World`/`ComponentReflection`/`CommandStack` **held in memory across every tool call in the session** (so `create_entity` then `set_component` compose, and undo/redo work across calls -- mirrors how Studio itself works: open, edit, explicit Save), then serves the protocol loop over real stdin/stdout until EOF. An unknown entity id or unregistered component name is reported inside a successful result (`{"ok": false}` / `{"value": null}`), never a protocol error -- `isError: true` is reserved for malformed/missing request parameters and an unrecognized tool name, matching this codebase's consistent never-throw-across-the-boundary convention. Caught and fixed two real bugs during plan-writing before any code was dispatched: `require_uint64`'s number-type check needed to accept both nlohmann's `number_integer` and `number_unsigned` JSON types (a naive `is_number_unsigned()`-only check would have rejected every ordinary small entity id parsed from real wire input, since nlohmann stores small non-negative JSON integers as `number_integer` by default); and `handle_request_line` needed an explicit non-object guard on both the top-level parsed JSON and the `params` field specifically, since a syntactically valid but wrongly-shaped line (a bare number, a `params` that isn't an object) would otherwise throw an uncaught `type_error` from nlohmann's `.value()`/`.at()` and crash the server instead of returning a clean JSON-RPC error. End-to-end verified manually (not just headless tests): `mosaic create` a fresh project, pipe `initialize`/`tools/list`/`tools/call(create_entity)`/`tools/call(save_scene)` JSON-RPC lines into `mosaic mcp <project>`, and confirm the newly created entity's id is actually written into `scenes/main.json` on disk -- the full round trip through the real CLI. **v1 scope is deliberately entity/component CRUD only** -- explicitly deferred, documented as future expansion: wrapping the existing `create`/`build`/`import`/`plugin` CLI verbs as MCP tools, and live-Studio attachment (an agent driving/observing a running GUI session in real time, mirroring the Blender Live-Link server) rather than only a headless project. 23 new tests (10 `mcp_tools`, 11 `mcp_protocol`, 2 `options`/CLI parsing) green across the full suite; zero new failures beyond the two already-known pre-existing flakes (`mosaic-threaded-script-test-flaky-segv`, the `--host`/`--connect` `ctest`-discovery quirk for `--`-prefixed `TEST_CASE` names -- see `catch2-test-names-no-dashes` in project memory). **Next Phase 6 sub-project: scene reasoning (2 of 7)** -- semantic queries over a live/saved scene, building on this slice's MCP server.
